# MCP sweep 2 Implementation Plan

> **Release branch (owner, 2026-10-04):** `release/workflow-graph`, cut from `origin/release/knowledge-graph` at f9e0cd18. Every task branch is cut from `origin/release/workflow-graph` and every PR goes `--base release/workflow-graph`. The Knowledge Graph release merges to `main` first; `main` is then merged INTO `release/workflow-graph`, never rebased.

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking. In the gisik orchestration, every task is ONE lane session. Follow `gisik-nokma-lanes/STEPS-common-2026-10-03.md`: numbered steps, each ending with a pushed commit + `CHECKPOINT task/<branch> <sha40> step <n>/<N>` + `HANDOFF-<branch>.md`; the sweep list + your own mutation pass before DONE.

**Goal:** five new MCP tools (`restore_note_version`, `close_tickets`, `get_notification_preferences`, `set_notification_preferences`, `list_saved_filters`) and three changed ones (`get_ticket` gains `notes`; `assign_ticket` and `create_ticket` gain `assignee_email` behind a two-call confirmation that says when a person will be ADDED AS A GUEST), with no server route, no migration and no event type.

**Architecture:**
- **Every tool wraps a route or event the app already uses.** Reads call the product's own routes (so its visibility rules apply unchanged); writes go through `envelopeFor` (actor = the session user, `origin: 'mcp'`) and the event door; the one new transport verb is `PUT` (`Session.put`).
- **Plumbing first (T2).** `Session.put` in every door, a by-index reader for a batch whose events commit one by one, `confirmOrPreview`'s parameter name, the `assign-outsider` `{ result }` unwrapping and the 429 sentence. Each is shipped with a control proving the old behaviour is unchanged.
- **Confirmations are stateless.** `close_tickets` confirms with the batch SIZE (`confirm_count`); `assignee_email` confirms with the resolved person's userId (`confirm_assignee`), re-resolved at call time. Nothing is remembered between calls (`directory.ts:10-12`).
- **A preview is a successful answer that wrote nothing.** Every task that previews asserts the RECORDED REQUESTS, not the reply.

**Tech stack:** TypeScript, zod 3, `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.30, Fastify (`app.inject`), vitest 3 (node), the shared test Postgres.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-03-mcp-sweep-2-design.md` (sections cited as §). Code was read at `origin/release/knowledge-graph` (tip `ec72d1f8`, 2026-10-03); every `file:line` below is from that tree. **Re-read each one at your base before editing**: the tip moves, and typed-links T4 (`link_tickets`, `get_ticket.links`), spec B (`linkedFrom`) and Graph Part 5 edit the same registry fixtures and the same `get_ticket` literal.

| | |
|---|---|

| **Release** | `release/workflow-graph` (cut 2026-10-04 from KG f9e0cd18, owner), the same release as Drafts and the Graph parts. |
| **Order inside this plan** | T1 first. T2 next. Then T3 and T7 are independent of each other and of T4-T6 (T7 needs only T2's helpers); T8 after T7. T4, T5, T6 each add tools and edit the same registry hunk, so they land **one after another** (T4, T5, T6), or rebase onto each other. T9 last. |
| **Shared hunks with other plans** | `packages/mcp-server/test/fixtures/tool-names.ts`, `tool-sweep.ts`, `register.test.ts:87-110` (every plan that adds a tool); the `get_ticket` return literal `read-tools.ts:165-169`; the tail of `Features.md` §14 and of `test-cases/14-mcp-server.md`. |

## Global Constraints

**Release and identity**
- **Branches:** every task branch is `task/mcp-sweep-2-t<n>`, cut from `origin/release/workflow-graph`; its PR has `--base release/workflow-graph`. Never `main`; never a second release branch.
- **Ledger:** plan id `mcp-sweep-2` (`scripts/check-task-ledger.mjs:263` strips the date from the file name). Rows are at the end of this plan.
- **No new event type, no new package, no server route, no migration.** So no vocabulary census (the event vocabulary does not move) and the gate stays at its task count (`scripts/ctl/verbs/gate.sh:55` is 41-only on the KG tip; read the count from the tip, never from this plan; nothing here adds a package with build/test/typecheck scripts). The plan adds test files to existing packages only.

**Catalogue**
- **File:** `test-cases/14-mcp-server.md`, new section `## 14.15 MCP sweep 2`; `Features.md` §14, a new marker after the MCP-everything bullets.
- **Ids:** exactly **14.600-14.659** (60 ids, all allocated by the spec's §12.1 table). **Do not mint 14.660 or above.** The O1 measurement (T1) is not a case and has no id.
- **Re-grep before allocating:** `refs=$(git for-each-ref refs/remotes/origin --format='%(refname:short)' | grep -E '^origin/(release|task|docs|rel|feat|fix)/'); git grep -n -E "(^|[^0-9.])14\.[6-9][0-9]{2}([^0-9]|$)" $refs -- test-cases Features.md apps packages docs scripts`. On 2026-10-03 (509 refs) the only hits were the spec's own lines; `## 14.15` was on no tip. If either moved, STOP and tell the planner.
- **Case lines are id lines:** `- ❌ **14.600 — …**` (column-0 bullet, id at the head; see `test-cases/18-notes.md:23`). Each starts ❌ and flips in the same commit as its covering test. Test names or comments carry the id. `scripts/fixtures/check-case-citations-baseline.json` has `"14-mcp-server.md": 0` and **stays 0**: only a flipped id must be cited, and every flip cites its test.
- **Parametrised cases (14.639-14.645, 14.647, 14.649, 14.652)** run over both `assign_ticket` and `create_ticket`. T7 covers `assign_ticket` and flips them to 🟡; T8 adds the `create_ticket` row to the same table and flips them to ✅.
- **After adding cases** confirm the count of id lines moved by exactly the number you added: `node scripts/check-case-ids.mjs && pnpm check:citations`.

**Registry pins: the count is re-read from the tip at execution, never hard-coded**
- Tasks T4, T5 and T6 add tools. Each edits, in its own commit: `tool-names.ts` and `tool-sweep.ts` (inside its own `begin`/`end` block), and `register.test.ts` 14.555 (the spelled-out `FINAL` list and its `toHaveLength(N)`).
- **N is "the length on YOUR base plus the tools this task adds".** Open the file, read the current `toHaveLength(...)`, add yours. Typed-links T4 (`link_tickets`), Graph Part 5 and spec D edit the same lines: do not write 64 or 68 from this plan, and when the base moved under you, re-read and re-add.
- 14.80, 14.81 and `exports.test.ts:22` compare `allTools` to `TOOL_NAMES`, so a tool added without its pin turns three tests red; that is the check, not a nuisance.
- **Prose counts move with the pin, in the same commit (planner ruling 2026-10-04).** T4, T5 and T6 each bump the tool count they change, in EVERY place it is written: the 14.555 `FINAL` list, its `toHaveLength(N)` and its title (`register.test.ts:87`); the count words in `Features.md` (`:3405` "sixty-three tools since MCP-everything", `:3407` "sixty-three since MCP-everything", `:3454` "Sixty-three tools in both doors", line numbers on `origin/release/workflow-graph`); and 14.555's case line `test-cases/14-mcp-server.md:938` ("the 63 tools"). Each bump is **+N over the tree you merge into; re-measure after a tipmerge**: 14.555 is a count pin that other plans share, so the number is `toHaveLength` on the merged tree, never one copied from this plan. In `Features.md` write it in words: `:3405` and `:3407` become "<count> (tools) since MCP-everything and MCP sweep 2", and `:3454` becomes "<Count> tools in both doors". The release branch never carries a stale count. T9 VERIFIES the final count (`rg -n -i "sixty-three|63 tools"` finds nothing, and every place agrees with `TOOL_NAMES.length`). It does not do the re-count.

**Decisions this plan made (the spec is silent or inconsistent)**
1. **`confirm_count` is `z.union([z.string(), z.number()])`, with no `.int()`.** §5.3 says `.int()`; CT-9 says `3.5` must answer the preview and send nothing. A zod error on `3.5` would be a thrown error, not a preview. The comparison is `String(given) === String(ticketIds.length)`.
2. **A miss on the email lookup is a thrown error** (`isError`), with the sentence `No user with that email.` and the hint, because nothing was done and an assistant must not read it as a success. Spec §7.3 says "answers"; the text is the spec's.
3. **`assign_ticket` call 2 reads the ticket only when it falls back to a preview** (to name it). Call 1 reads it first, before any lookup (AE-11). So a ticket deleted between the calls reaches the server's own refusal and, for an outsider, the compensation (people.ts:210-218).
4. **`NO_TRANSFER` and `NO_CLIENT_TRANSFER` gain `put`** (their names stay). Most test `Session` literals spread them, so one edit covers them; the hand-built ones are listed in T2.
5. **The byte-equality test against the app's sentences (AE-16) lives in `apps/server/test/mcp/`**, not in `packages/mcp-server`: that package must not depend on app-core. The MCP keeps its own copies of the strings. That test needs app-core BUILT.
6. **README:** `packages/mcp-server/README.md:66-70` omits all 39 MCP-everything tools. T9 does NOT write a census over `TOOL_NAMES` (it would be red on day one); it adds a paragraph for this plan's five new tools and three changed ones and checks those. The staleness is reported as a gap.
7. **`close_tickets` sits right after `reopen_ticket` in `tool-names.ts`; `restore_note_version` right after `restore_note`; the three discovery tools right after `mark_notifications_read`.** Mid-array insertions, so they do not collide with tools other plans append at the end of a block.

**Copy rules**
- The app's own sentences are reproduced exactly (`packages/app-core/src/people/copy.ts:11-12`, `:18-20`; the "ADDED AS A GUEST" wording is the spec's §7.3). A rewording in the app fails 14.651.
- Do not put a spaced em dash (` — `) or a middle dot (` · `) in any new string literal in `packages/mcp-server/src` (the project's ui-copy scan, `packages/app-core/test/copy/ui-copy.test.ts:90-139`, forbids them in UI strings; keep the MCP's strings clean for the same reason).
- Test files and `Features.md` follow the house voice: no emoji except the legend markers.

**Tests and tooling**
- **Node 22** for `pnpm install` in a worktree.
- **Every test run goes through the slot door:** `SR=/Users/kiro/my_projects/gisik-nokma-lanes/slot-run.sh; "$SR" <label> -- <command>`. It logs, tails and returns the real exit code.
- **Never pipe a test or build run into `head`** (`| tail -N` or the slot door's own log). No `pkill`/`killall`, ever; stop a process by exact pid only.
- **Build before suites.** The server suites import `@gisik-nokma/mcp-server`'s `dist`; build it after every change in that package: `pnpm --filter @gisik-nokma/mcp-server build`. T7/T8's AE-16 test also imports app-core: `pnpm --filter @gisik-nokma/app-core build` (after `domain`, `rule-engine`, `sync-client`, `ui` if their dist is stale).
- **`vitest` does not typecheck.** The RED/GREEN for any `Session`/`GisikClient` shape change is `pnpm --filter @gisik-nokma/mcp-server typecheck` AND `pnpm --filter @gisik-nokma/server typecheck`; put both in that task's run list.
- **An app-core online/sync change** would require the server integration files WHOLE; this plan makes none. **An editor/domain change** would require regenerating the mobile editor bundle; this plan makes none.
- **Every run quoted in a report is named LOCAL.** CI runs only on the release-to-`main` PR.
- **Controls live in the same `it()`** as the case they guard: a refusal test also proves the same call with one input changed is accepted.
- **Features.md and `test-cases/` change in the same commit as the code.** Each task writes only below its own anchors (T1 seeds them).
- **UI:** none. There is no `frontend-design` step in this plan.
- Gate task count and every pinned member/registry count are MEASURED on the tip at execution and pinned in the same task that changes them; never copy a number from this plan.

## Review Focus

1. **A preview that wrote something.** `close_tickets` without the exact count, and `assign_ticket` / `create_ticket` without the exact userId, must send NO event, NO `assign-outsider` and (for a create) leave no ticket. The tests assert the recorded requests and the ledger, never the reply: 14.624 (T5), 14.639 and 14.654 (T7, T8).
2. **A confirm spelled almost right.** A trailing space, the email, the display name, the wrong case, a count of `"03"`, `3.5` or a different batch size: each answers a fresh preview and writes nothing; the exact value, in the same `it`, writes. 14.624 (T5), 14.643 (T7).
3. **A person who is already a guest.** A lookup answers `inWorkspace: true` for any role, a guest included, so the tool shows "already in this workspace" and writes an ordinary event (no second grant, no demotion). Pinned in the hosted tests 14.652 (T7) with a guest account.
4. **A restore the gate refuses** (a plain member who is not the note's author). The gate's own sentence reaches the assistant verbatim, with no `event 0` prefix, and nothing is written: 14.615 (T4).
5. **Two different people behind one network key.** On the hosted door the lookup and `/mcp` budgets are keyed to the address the proxy saw, so the second person's first preview can be a 429. The assistant must be told in a sentence, not `answered 429`: 14.648 (T7); the measurement is T1.

### Task 1: Measure the lookup budget (spec O1) and open the catalogue (no case ids)

**Files:**
- Create: `apps/server/test/mcp/mcp-lookup-budget.test.ts`
- Modify:
  - `test-cases/14-mcp-server.md` (append the `14.15` section at the end)
  - `Features.md` (insert the `mcp-sweep-2` marker and anchors after the last MCP-everything bullet, the one before `## 15. Notes`, `:3452` on the tip)
  - `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-03-mcp-sweep-2-design.md` §13 (the `O1` row, if the spec file is on your base; otherwise the result goes in the HANDOFF only)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `hostedSession(app, outer, accessToken, userId)` and `OuterCaller` (`apps/server/src/mcp/in-process-fetch.ts:5`, `:50-65`); `buildOAuthApp(db, overrides)`, `hostedToken(app, pool, userId)` (`apps/server/test/helpers/oauth.ts:12`, `:56`); `signInWithPhone` (`helpers/session.ts:39`); `SecurityOptions.rateLimit` / `trustProxy` / `now` (`apps/server/src/app.ts:256-295`, `:300-330`); `signInMaxFor` (`apps/server/src/http/rate-limit.ts:343`).
- Produces: nothing that later tasks import. The deliverable is a pinned set of numbers and a paragraph for the owner.

**What this task answers (spec §13 O1).** The lookup route `POST /v1/workspaces/:id/people-lookup` is charged to the same per-key bucket as invitation minting (`app.ts:691-705`: `inviteLimiter.check(`invite:${clientKey}`)`), with the budget `RATE_LIMIT_AUTH_MAX` (`index.ts:216`; `deploy/do/app.yaml:185-186` sets 10 per `RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SEC` 60). One by-email assignment makes TWO lookups (call 1 and the re-resolution in call 2). On the hosted door `/mcp` itself is charged to the mcp bucket with the SAME budget (`app.ts:719-728`, `index.ts:219`), and the key is the address the proxy saw (`routes/mcp-http.ts:71-73` passes `request.ip` and `x-forwarded-for` to `hostedSession`; `rate-limit.ts:113-125` takes the LAST forwarded entry under `trustProxy`). The owner decides whether the tool needs a tighter cap or its own bucket; **this task changes no server code and decides nothing.**

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the measurement**

  `apps/server/test/mcp/mcp-lookup-budget.test.ts`. The numbers are READ from `deploy/do/app.yaml`, so a changed deploy value fails this file until it is re-measured. Only the invite and mcp buckets are under measurement; the other three are set high so the setup (sign-ins, `/oauth/register`, `/oauth/token`) cannot trip them.

  ```ts
  import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
  import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
  import type { FastifyInstance } from 'fastify';
  import { newUlid } from '@gisik-nokma/domain';
  import { HttpError, type LazySession } from '@gisik-nokma/mcp-server';
  import type { SecurityOptions } from '../../src/app.js';
  import { hostedSession } from '../../src/mcp/in-process-fetch.js';
  import { buildOAuthApp, hostedToken } from '../helpers/oauth.js';
  import { createTestDatabase, type TestDatabase } from '../helpers/db.js';
  import { signInWithPhone } from '../helpers/session.js';

  /**
   * MCP sweep 2 T1, spec §13 O1. NOT a catalogue case (the 60 ids are allocated): a measurement of the
   * budget the by-email tools will spend, against the numbers the deployment ships. Run LOCALLY.
   */
  let db: TestDatabase;
  beforeAll(async () => {
    db = await createTestDatabase();
  });
  afterAll(async () => {
    await db.drop();
  });

  const yaml = readFileSync(new URL('../../../../deploy/do/app.yaml', import.meta.url), 'utf8');
  function deployed(key: string): number {
    const hit = new RegExp(`- key: ${key}\\s+value: "(\\d+)"`).exec(yaml);
    if (hit === null) throw new Error(`deploy/do/app.yaml no longer sets ${key} as a quoted number; re-read it before trusting this measurement`);
    return Number(hit[1]);
  }
  const BUDGET = deployed('RATE_LIMIT_AUTH_MAX');
  const WINDOW = deployed('RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SEC');
  const EVENTS = deployed('RATE_LIMIT_EVENTS_MAX');

  type Person = { userId: string; token: string; hosted: string };
  type World = { app: FastifyInstance; clock: { t: number }; ws: string; a: Person; b: Person };

  async function world(trustProxy: boolean, tail: string): Promise<World> {
    const clock = { t: 1_800_000_000_000 };
    const security: SecurityOptions = {
      webOrigin: null,
      trustProxy,
      now: () => clock.t,
      // authMax and signInMax are NOT under measurement: high, so sign-in cannot trip them.
      rateLimit: { authMax: 1000, eventsMax: EVENTS, inviteMax: BUDGET, mcpMax: BUDGET, signInMax: 1000, windowSec: WINDOW },
    };
    const { app, otp } = buildOAuthApp(db, { security });
    await app.ready();
    const owner = await signInWithPhone(app, otp, `+9111000${tail}1`);
    const other = await signInWithPhone(app, otp, `+9111000${tail}2`);
    const found = await app.inject({ method: 'POST', url: '/v1/workspaces', headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${owner.token}` }, payload: { name: 'Budget Co' } });
    const ws = (found.json() as { workspaceId: string }).workspaceId;
    // The second person is a real MEMBER (the lookup refuses a guest and a stranger with a 404 AFTER the limiter).
    const granted = await app.inject({
      method: 'POST', url: '/v1/events', headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${owner.token}` },
      payload: { events: [{ id: newUlid(), workspaceId: ws, entity: { kind: 'membership', id: `${ws}:${other.userId}` }, type: 'membership.granted', version: 1,
        payload: { userId: other.userId, role: 'member' }, actorId: owner.userId, origin: 'web', clientTime: new Date().toISOString() }] },
    });
    expect((granted.json() as { results: Array<{ status: string }> }).results[0]?.status).toBe('committed');
    const a = { userId: owner.userId, token: owner.token, hosted: await hostedToken(app, db.pool, owner.userId) };
    const b = { userId: other.userId, token: other.token, hosted: await hostedToken(app, db.pool, other.userId) };
    clock.t += WINDOW * 1000 + 1; // the setup spent the mcp bucket of the test's own address: reopen every window
    return { app, clock, ws, a, b };
  }

  const lookup = (s: LazySession, ws: string, i: number) =>
    s.post(`/v1/workspaces/${ws}/people-lookup`, { email: `nobody-${i}@example.test` });

  /** Lookups until the first 429: how many were allowed, and the refusal. */
  async function drain(s: LazySession, ws: string): Promise<{ allowed: number; refused: HttpError }> {
    await s.ready();
    for (let i = 0; i < BUDGET + 5; i++) {
      try {
        await lookup(s, ws, i);
      } catch (error) {
        if (error instanceof HttpError && error.status === 429) return { allowed: i, refused: error };
        throw error;
      }
    }
    throw new Error('the lookup was never limited');
  }

  const OUTER = (ip: string, xff?: string) => ({ ip, xff });

  describe('O1 the lookup budget a by-email assignment spends', () => {
    it('O1.1 one network key gets exactly the deployed budget of lookups per window, then 429 with a wait; the window reopens', async () => {
      const w = await world(false, '3011');
      const s = hostedSession(w.app, OUTER('198.51.100.10'), w.a.token, w.a.userId);
      const first = await drain(s, w.ws);
      expect(first.allowed).toBe(BUDGET);
      expect(first.refused.body).toMatchObject({ error: 'rate_limited' });
      const wait = (first.refused.body as { retryAfterSeconds: number }).retryAfterSeconds;
      expect(wait).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(1);
      expect(wait).toBeLessThanOrEqual(WINDOW);
      // CONTROL, same `it`: the window reopens, and the lookup is allowed again.
      w.clock.t += WINDOW * 1000 + 1;
      await expect(lookup(s, w.ws, 99)).resolves.toBeDefined();
      await w.app.close();
    });

    it('O1.2 the lookup spends the SAME bucket as minting an invitation: a spent key cannot invite; another key can', async () => {
      const w = await world(false, '3012');
      const invite = (s: LazySession) => s.post(`/v1/workspaces/${w.ws}/invites`, { channel: 'email', address: 'someone@example.test', role: 'member' });
      const spent = hostedSession(w.app, OUTER('198.51.100.20'), w.a.token, w.a.userId);
      const fresh = hostedSession(w.app, OUTER('198.51.100.21'), w.a.token, w.a.userId);
      await drain(spent, w.ws);
      await expect(invite(spent)).rejects.toMatchObject({ status: 429 });
      await fresh.ready();
      await expect(invite(fresh)).resolves.toBeDefined(); // CONTROL: the route works when its bucket is not spent
      await w.app.close();
    });

    it('O1.3 two different people behind one forwarded address share ONE bucket (the key is the address, never the person); another address does not', async () => {
      const w = await world(true, '3013');
      const sameEgress = OUTER('10.0.0.1', '203.0.113.9'); // the proxy hop, then the address it saw
      const sa = hostedSession(w.app, sameEgress, w.a.token, w.a.userId);
      const sb = hostedSession(w.app, sameEgress, w.b.token, w.b.userId);
      await drain(sa, w.ws);
      await sb.ready();
      await expect(lookup(sb, w.ws, 1)).rejects.toMatchObject({ status: 429 }); // person B starved by person A
      const elsewhere = hostedSession(w.app, OUTER('10.0.0.1', '203.0.113.10'), w.b.token, w.b.userId);
      await elsewhere.ready();
      await expect(lookup(elsewhere, w.ws, 2)).resolves.toBeDefined(); // CONTROL: same hop, another forwarded address
      await w.app.close();
    });

    it('O1.4 /mcp itself is charged to its own bucket at the same budget, per forwarded address, whatever the tool or the person', async () => {
      const w = await world(true, '3014');
      const call = (token: string, xff: string) =>
        w.app.inject({
          method: 'POST', url: '/mcp', remoteAddress: '10.0.0.1',
          headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json', accept: 'application/json, text/event-stream', 'mcp-protocol-version': '2025-06-18', authorization: `Bearer ${token}`, 'x-forwarded-for': xff },
          payload: { jsonrpc: '2.0', id: 1, method: 'tools/call', params: { name: 'list_workspaces', arguments: {} } },
        });
      let allowed = 0;
      for (let i = 0; i < BUDGET + 5; i++) {
        const res = await call(i % 2 === 0 ? w.a.hosted : w.b.hosted, '203.0.113.30'); // two people alternate on one address
        if (res.statusCode === 429) break;
        expect(res.statusCode).toBe(200);
        allowed += 1;
      }
      expect(allowed).toBe(BUDGET);
      expect((await call(w.a.hosted, '203.0.113.31')).statusCode).toBe(200); // CONTROL: another address
      await w.app.close();
    });
  });
  ```

  Run (LOCAL): `"$SR" ms2-t1 -- pnpm --filter @gisik-nokma/server exec vitest run test/mcp/mcp-lookup-budget.test.ts --maxWorkers=2`.

If an assertion fails, the READING (not the code) is wrong: read the failing request's status, fix the assertion to the measured fact, and say so in the HANDOFF. If `world()` itself fails at `hostedToken` with a 429, the mcp bucket of `127.0.0.1` is spent by the setup: raise nothing, move the `clock.t` bump before the second `hostedToken`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the fixture and the failing tests**

  Re-read each `file:line` above at your base. Then create `packages/mcp-server/test/fixtures/recording-session.ts` (the shared fake of T3-T8: it records EVERY request and answers by route, so a test can assert what was NOT sent):

  ```ts
  import { HttpError } from '../../src/http-client.js';
  import type { Session } from '../../src/session.js';
  import { NO_TRANSFER } from './fake-session.js';

  /** One request a tool made. `EVENTS` is `postEvents`; its `body` is the array of events. */
  export type Recorded = { method: 'GET' | 'POST' | 'PUT' | 'EVENTS'; path: string; body?: unknown };
  /** A canned answer, or a function of (body, path) that answers or throws (an `HttpError`, say). */
  export type Route = unknown | ((body: unknown, path: string) => unknown | Promise<unknown>);

  const COMMITTED = { status: 'committed', eventId: 'e', seq: 1, serverTime: '2026-10-03T00:00:00.000Z' };

  /**
   * A `Session` that records every request and answers by route (MCP sweep 2). Keys are `"GET /path"`,
   * `"POST /path"`, `"PUT /path"`; a key matches the full path or the path before its `?`. A request with no
   * route is a 404 (`HttpError`), as the product answers a thing the caller may not see. `postEvents` answers
   * one committed result per event unless `opts.events` says otherwise.
   */
  export function recordingSession(opts: {
    userId?: string;
    routes?: Record<string, Route>;
    events?: (events: Array<Record<string, unknown>>) => unknown;
  } = {}): { session: Session; requests: Recorded[] } {
    const requests: Recorded[] = [];
    const answer = async (method: 'GET' | 'POST' | 'PUT', path: string, body?: unknown): Promise<unknown> => {
      requests.push(body === undefined ? { method, path } : { method, path, body });
      const hit = [`${method} ${path}`, `${method} ${path.split('?')[0]}`].find((key) => opts.routes !== undefined && Object.hasOwn(opts.routes, key));
      if (hit === undefined || opts.routes === undefined) throw new HttpError(404, { error: 'not_found' });
      const route = opts.routes[hit];
      return typeof route === 'function' ? await (route as (b: unknown, p: string) => unknown)(body, path) : route;
    };
    const session: Session = {
      ...NO_TRANSFER,
      userId: () => opts.userId ?? '01J0A5ER0000000000000000AA',
      get: (path) => answer('GET', path),
      post: (path, body) => answer('POST', path, body),
      put: (path, body) => answer('PUT', path, body),
      postEvents: async (events) => {
        requests.push({ method: 'EVENTS', path: '/v1/events', body: events });
        const list = events as Array<Record<string, unknown>>;
        return opts.events !== undefined ? opts.events(list) : { results: list.map(() => COMMITTED) };
      },
    };
    return { session, requests };
  }

  /** Every `postEvents` batch, in order. */
  export function eventBatches(requests: Recorded[]): Array<Array<Record<string, unknown>>> {
    return requests.filter((r) => r.method === 'EVENTS').map((r) => r.body as Array<Record<string, unknown>>);
  }
  ```

  `packages/mcp-server/test/put.test.ts` (14.600, package half):

  ```ts
  import { mkdtempSync } from 'node:fs';
  import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
  import { join } from 'node:path';
  import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
  import { DEFAULT_POLL_INTERVAL_MS } from '../src/config.js';
  import { createGisikClient, HttpError, type GisikClient } from '../src/http-client.js';
  import { lazySession } from '../src/lazy-session.js';
  import { createSession } from '../src/session.js';
  import { writeCachedToken } from '../src/token-cache.js';
  import { NO_CLIENT_TRANSFER } from './fixtures/fake-session.js';

  /** MCP sweep 2 T2, case 14.600 (package half; the hosted door is `apps/server/test/mcp/in-process-put.test.ts`). */
  const config = () => ({ serverUrl: 'http://server', tokenPath: join(mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'gisik-put-')), 'mcp-token'), pollIntervalMs: DEFAULT_POLL_INTERVAL_MS });

  describe('Session.put', () => {
    it('14.600 reaches the server as a PUT with the bearer token and a JSON body through the client, the session (one re-login on a 401) and the lazy wrapper; post is still a POST', async () => {
      const seen: Array<{ method: string; url: string; auth: string | null; type: string | null; body: string }> = [];
      const stub: typeof fetch = async (input, init) => {
        const req = new Request(input as string, init);
        seen.push({ method: req.method, url: req.url, auth: req.headers.get('authorization'), type: req.headers.get('content-type'), body: await req.text() });
        return new Response(JSON.stringify({ mutedKinds: ['mention'] }), { status: 200, headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' } });
      };
      const client = createGisikClient('http://server', stub);
      expect(await client.put('/v1/me/notification-preferences', { mutedKinds: ['mention'] }, 'tok')).toEqual({ mutedKinds: ['mention'] });
      expect(seen[0]).toEqual({ method: 'PUT', url: 'http://server/v1/me/notification-preferences', auth: 'Bearer tok', type: 'application/json', body: '{"mutedKinds":["mention"]}' });
      // CONTROL, same `it`: post is untouched by the new verb.
      await client.post('/v1/x', { a: 1 }, 'tok');
      expect(seen[1]).toMatchObject({ method: 'POST', body: '{"a":1}' });

      // The stdio door: createSession re-authenticates ONCE on a 401 and repeats the PUT (the `get` rule, `session.ts:56-65`).
      const cfg = config();
      await writeCachedToken(cfg.tokenPath, { token: 'stale', userId: 'old' });
      const tokens: string[] = [];
      const fake: GisikClient = {
        ...NO_CLIENT_TRANSFER,
        get: async () => ({}),
        post: async (path) =>
          path === '/v1/mcp/device/code'
            ? { deviceCode: 'd', userCode: 'BCDF-GHJK', verificationUri: 'http://web/a', expiresInSec: 600, pollIntervalSec: 1 }
            : { status: 'issued', token: 'fresh', userId: '01J0USER0000000000000000BB' },
        put: async (_path, body, token) => {
          tokens.push(token);
          if (token === 'stale') throw new HttpError(401, { error: 'unauthenticated' });
          return { stored: body };
        },
      };
      const session = await createSession({ config: cfg, client: fake, prompt: () => {}, sleep: async () => {}, now: () => 0 });
      expect(await session.put('/v1/me/notification-preferences', { mutedKinds: [] })).toEqual({ stored: { mutedKinds: [] } });
      expect(tokens).toEqual(['stale', 'fresh']);

      // The deferred wrapper waits for the credential, then forwards.
      let opened = 0;
      const lazy = lazySession(async () => {
        opened += 1;
        return session;
      });
      expect(await lazy.put('/v1/x', { z: 1 })).toEqual({ stored: { z: 1 } });
      expect(opened).toBe(1);
    });
  });
  ```

  ```ts
      expect(await session.post('/v1/echo', { a: 2 })).toEqual({ method: 'POST', body: { a: 2 } }); // CONTROL
      for (const verb of ['userId', 'get', 'post', 'put', 'postEvents', 'getBytes', 'postMultipart'] as const) {
  ```

  Kills (14.600): (a) `createGisikClient().put` calls `request('POST', …)` → `seen[0].method` is `POST`; (b) `createSession().put` calls `deps.client.put(path, body, cached.token)` without `withReauth` → the stale token's 401 escapes and `tokens` is `['stale']`; (c) `hostedSession`'s `put` is `(path, body) => client.post(path, body, accessToken)` → the hosted echo answers `{ method: 'POST', … }`. (The hosted verb loop reads the `LazySession` wrapper, so it pins the wrapper's verbs, not `hostedSession`'s inner literal; (c) is what pins the inner `put`.)

  `packages/mcp-server/test/session-census.test.ts` (14.601). The census is on VERBS, not on text: a regex over source would flag the sweep's `Partial<Session>` overrides and pass a literal that only looks complete. The strict check for hand-built literals is the compiler (`tsconfig.typecheck.json` of both packages includes `test`); this file pins that every REAL builder carries every verb and that the census can see a short one.

  ```ts
  import { mkdtempSync } from 'node:fs';
  import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
  import { join } from 'node:path';
  import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
  import { DEFAULT_POLL_INTERVAL_MS } from '../src/config.js';
  import type { GisikClient } from '../src/http-client.js';
  import { lazySession } from '../src/lazy-session.js';
  import { createSession, type Session } from '../src/session.js';
  import { writeCachedToken } from '../src/token-cache.js';
  import { NO_CLIENT_TRANSFER, NO_TRANSFER } from './fixtures/fake-session.js';

  const VERBS = ['userId', 'get', 'post', 'put', 'postEvents', 'getBytes', 'postMultipart'] as const satisfies readonly (keyof Session)[];
  /** Red at TYPECHECK when `Session` gains a verb this list does not name: add it here, then to every builder. */
  type Unlisted = Exclude<keyof Session, (typeof VERBS)[number]>;
  const everyVerbListed: [Unlisted] extends [never] ? true : never = true;

  function missingVerbs(session: object): string[] {
    return VERBS.filter((verb) => typeof (session as Record<string, unknown>)[verb] !== 'function');
  }

  describe('every Session builder carries every verb', () => {
    it('14.601 createSession, lazySession and the NO_* fixtures have all of them, and the census names a builder that lacks one', async () => {
      expect(everyVerbListed).toBe(true);
      const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'gisik-census-'));
      const tokenPath = join(dir, 'mcp-token');
      await writeCachedToken(tokenPath, { token: 't', userId: 'U' });
      const client: GisikClient = { ...NO_CLIENT_TRANSFER, get: async () => ({}), post: async () => ({}), put: async () => ({}) };
      const real = await createSession({ config: { serverUrl: 'http://server', tokenPath, pollIntervalMs: DEFAULT_POLL_INTERVAL_MS }, client, prompt: () => {}, sleep: async () => {}, now: () => 0 });
      expect(missingVerbs(real)).toEqual([]);
      expect(missingVerbs(lazySession(async () => real))).toEqual([]);
      expect(missingVerbs({ userId: () => 'U', get: async () => ({}), post: async () => ({}), postEvents: async () => ({}), ...NO_TRANSFER })).toEqual([]);
      expect(Object.keys(NO_CLIENT_TRANSFER).sort()).toEqual(['getBytes', 'postMultipart', 'put']);
      // CONTROL, same `it`: the census is not vacuous.
      const { put: _dropped, ...short } = real;
      expect(missingVerbs(short)).toEqual(['put']);
    });
  });
  ```

  Kills (14.601): (a) `lazySession` returns no `put` member → `missingVerbs(lazySession(…))` is `['put']`; (b) `NO_TRANSFER` keeps its old `Pick<…, 'getBytes' | 'postMultipart'>` body without `put` → the spread literal's census is `['put']`; (c) `NO_CLIENT_TRANSFER` without `put` → the key list is `['getBytes', 'postMultipart']`. (`expect(everyVerbListed).toBe(true)` is a runtime constant: it can only go red at TYPECHECK, when `Session` gains a verb `VERBS` does not name.)

  `packages/mcp-server/test/submit-by-index.test.ts` (14.602):

  ```ts
  import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
  import { HttpError } from '../src/http-client.js';
  import { envelopeFor, resultError, submitBatch, submitBatchByIndex, submitOne, type EventResult } from '../src/tools/submit.js';
  import { recordingSession } from './fixtures/recording-session.js';

  const OK: EventResult = { status: 'committed', eventId: 'e1', seq: 1, serverTime: '2026-10-03T00:00:00.000Z' };
  const REFUSED: EventResult = { status: 'refused', eventId: 'e2', rule: 'close-requires-terminal', reason: 'A ticket can only be closed from a terminal state; "Open" is not one.' };
  const UNKNOWN = 'what happened is unknown';
  const three = (s: ReturnType<typeof recordingSession>['session']) =>
    ['A', 'B', 'C'].map((id) => envelopeFor(s, 'W', { kind: 'ticket', id }, 'ticket.closed', {}));

  describe('submitBatchByIndex', () => {
    it('14.602 returns every outcome index-aligned without throwing on a refusal; throws only on a short, holed or missing answer; submitBatch still throws on the same input', async () => {
      const partial = recordingSession({ events: () => ({ results: [OK, REFUSED, { ...OK, eventId: 'e3', seq: 3 }] }) });
      const results = await submitBatchByIndex(partial.session, three(partial.session), UNKNOWN);
      expect(results.map((r) => r.status)).toEqual(['committed', 'refused', 'committed']);
      expect(results[1]).toEqual(REFUSED);
      // CONTROL, same `it`: the old reader is unchanged, and spells the refusal with its index.
      await expect(submitBatch(partial.session, three(partial.session))).rejects.toThrow(`event 1 refused by close-requires-terminal: ${REFUSED.reason}`);
      // `invalid` is an outcome too.
      const invalid = recordingSession({ events: () => ({ results: [{ status: 'invalid', eventId: null, code: 'malformed', message: 'x' }, OK, OK] }) });
      expect((await submitBatchByIndex(invalid.session, three(invalid.session), UNKNOWN))[0]).toMatchObject({ status: 'invalid', code: 'malformed' });

      for (const [label, answer] of [
        ['a short answer', { results: [OK, OK] }],
        ['a holed answer', { results: [OK, null, OK] }],
        ['an element with no status', { results: [OK, {}, OK] }],
        ['no results at all', {}],
        ['a null body', null],
      ] as const) {
        const s = recordingSession({ events: () => answer });
        await expect(submitBatchByIndex(s.session, three(s.session), UNKNOWN), label).rejects.toThrow(UNKNOWN);
      }
      // A rejected call is the same: nothing is claimed, and the cause rides along in brackets.
      const down = recordingSession({ events: () => { throw new HttpError(500, { error: 'boom' }); } });
      await expect(submitBatchByIndex(down.session, three(down.session), UNKNOWN)).rejects.toThrow(`${UNKNOWN} (gisik-nokma answered 500)`);
      // CONTROL: a complete answer resolves.
      const whole = recordingSession();
      expect(await submitBatchByIndex(whole.session, three(whole.session), UNKNOWN)).toHaveLength(3);
    });

    it('14.602 resultError spells a refusal and an invalid result exactly as submitOne does', async () => {
      const refused = recordingSession({ events: () => ({ results: [REFUSED] }) });
      const event = envelopeFor(refused.session, 'W', { kind: 'ticket', id: 'A' }, 'ticket.closed', {});
      // LITERALS, not `resultError(REFUSED).message`: after the refactor `submitOne` calls `resultError`, so comparing the two
      // with each other passes whatever the template says.
      const SPELLED = `refused by close-requires-terminal: ${REFUSED.reason}`;
      expect(resultError(REFUSED)!.message).toBe(SPELLED);
      await expect(submitOne(refused.session, event)).rejects.toThrow(SPELLED);
      expect(resultError({ status: 'invalid', eventId: null, code: 'malformed', message: 'bad payload' })!.message).toBe('malformed: bad payload');
      expect(resultError(OK)).toBeNull(); // CONTROL
    });
  });
  ```

  Kills (14.602):
  - first `it`: (a) `submitBatchByIndex` drops `results.length !== events.length` → the `short answer` row resolves; (b) it drops `!results.every(isEventResult)` → the `holed answer` and `no status` rows resolve; (c) it throws on a `refused` element (copying `submitBatch`'s `forEach`) → the partial batch rejects; (d) its `catch` rethrows the error unwrapped → the 500 row's message lacks `what happened is unknown`.
  - second `it`: (a) `resultError`'s refused template drops `by ` or the rule → the `SPELLED` literal fails; (b) the invalid template becomes `${code} ${message}` → `'malformed: bad payload'` fails; (c) `resultError` returns an `Error` for `committed` → the control fails.

  ```ts
  import { confirmOrPreview } from '../src/tools/preview.js';

  const explain = async () => ({ sentence: 'Does the thing.', counts: { n: 2 } });

  describe('confirmOrPreview', () => {
    it('14.603 names the confirm parameter it was given; the default is confirm_name; the comparison is exact', async () => {
      expect(await confirmOrPreview('KEY-1', undefined, explain)).toEqual({
        preview: true, wouldChange: 'Does the thing.', counts: { n: 2 }, nothingChanged: true, confirm: 'confirm_name must be "KEY-1"',
      });
      expect((await confirmOrPreview('01J0USER', undefined, explain, 'confirm_assignee'))?.confirm).toBe('confirm_assignee must be "01J0USER"');
      expect((await confirmOrPreview('3', undefined, explain, 'confirm_count'))?.confirm).toBe('confirm_count must be "3"');
      for (const wrong of ['KEY-1 ', ' KEY-1', 'key-1', 'KEY-2', '']) expect(await confirmOrPreview('KEY-1', wrong, explain), JSON.stringify(wrong)).not.toBeNull();
      // CONTROL, same `it`: the exact value goes ahead, whatever the parameter is called.
  ```

  `packages/mcp-server/test/submit-mappers.test.ts` (14.604):

  ```ts
  import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
  import { HttpError } from '../src/http-client.js';
  import type { Session } from '../src/session.js';
  import { envelopeFor, postJson, postOutsider, putJson, rateLimitedError } from '../src/tools/submit.js';
  import { recordingSession } from './fixtures/recording-session.js';

  const throwing = (status: number, body: unknown): Session => ({
    ...recordingSession().session,
    post: async () => { throw new HttpError(status, body); },
    put: async () => { throw new HttpError(status, body); },
  });
  const COMMITTED = { status: 'committed', eventId: 'e', seq: 4, serverTime: '2026-10-03T00:00:00.000Z' };
  const REFUSED = { status: 'refused', eventId: 'e', rule: 'permission.role', reason: 'That person is not in this workspace.' };
  const claim = (s: Session) => envelopeFor(s, 'W', { kind: 'ticket', id: 'T' }, 'ticket.assigned', { assigneeId: 'U2' });

  describe('the response mappers', () => {
    it('14.604 putJson speaks exactly as postJson for 400 and 422, and both leave 404, 429 and 500 untouched', async () => {
      // Each row's sentence is spelled out: `postJson` and `putJson` now share `wordsFor`, so `viaPut === viaPost` alone passes
      // whatever `wordsFor` says. The literal pins the words; the equality pins that postJson did not move in the refactor.
      for (const [status, body, words] of [
        [400, { error: 'invalid_notification_preferences', message: 'Not a preferences document.' }, 'invalid_notification_preferences: Not a preferences document.'],
        [400, {}, 'gisik-nokma answered 400: {}'],
        [422, REFUSED, 'refused by permission.role: That person is not in this workspace.'],
        [422, { status: 'invalid', code: 'malformed', message: 'bad' }, 'malformed: bad'],
      ] as const) {
        const viaPost = await postJson(throwing(status, body), '/x', {}).then(() => 'resolved', (e: Error) => e.message);
        const viaPut = await putJson(throwing(status, body), '/x', {}).then(() => 'resolved', (e: Error) => e.message);
        expect(viaPut, `${status} ${JSON.stringify(body)}`).toBe(words);
        expect(viaPost, `${status} ${JSON.stringify(body)}`).toBe(words);
      }
      for (const status of [404, 429, 500]) {
        await expect(putJson(throwing(status, { error: 'x' }), '/x', {})).rejects.toMatchObject({ status });
        await expect(postJson(throwing(status, { error: 'x' }), '/x', {})).rejects.toMatchObject({ status });
      }
      // CONTROL: a success passes through.
      expect(await putJson(recordingSession({ routes: { 'PUT /x': { ok: true } } }).session, '/x', {})).toEqual({ ok: true });
    });

    it('14.604 postOutsider answers the unwrapped result on 200 and on 422, throws the server\'s words for a 400, and lets a 404 through', async () => {
      const ok = { ...recordingSession().session, post: async () => ({ result: COMMITTED }) };
      expect(await postOutsider(ok, 'W', 'U2', claim(ok))).toEqual(COMMITTED);
      // 422 { result } IS an answer (`online/people.ts:48-63` returns it, never throws it).
      const refused = throwing(422, { result: REFUSED });
      expect(await postOutsider(refused, 'W', 'U2', claim(refused))).toEqual(REFUSED);
      // A 422 with no `result` is not an answer: it is worded like any other 422.
      await expect(postOutsider(throwing(422, { nope: true }), 'W', 'U2', claim(refused))).rejects.toThrow('gisik-nokma answered 422: {"nope":true}');
      await expect(postOutsider(throwing(400, { error: 'bad_request', message: 'The claim must be your own ticket.created or ticket.assigned in this workspace, naming this person as the assignee.' }), 'W', 'U2', claim(refused)))
        .rejects.toThrow('bad_request: The claim must be your own ticket.created or ticket.assigned');
      await expect(postOutsider(throwing(404, { error: 'not_found' }), 'W', 'U2', claim(refused))).rejects.toMatchObject({ status: 404 });
      // 200 without a result is a fault, not a success.
      await expect(postOutsider({ ...ok, post: async () => ({}) }, 'W', 'U2', claim(ok))).rejects.toThrow(/without a result/);
      // The request: the route, and { userId, event } exactly.
      const rec = recordingSession({ routes: { 'POST /v1/workspaces/W%201/assign-outsider': { result: COMMITTED } } });
      await postOutsider(rec.session, 'W 1', 'U2', claim(rec.session));
      expect(rec.requests[0]).toMatchObject({ method: 'POST', path: '/v1/workspaces/W%201/assign-outsider', body: { userId: 'U2', event: { type: 'ticket.assigned', origin: 'mcp' } } });
      expect(Object.keys(rec.requests[0]!.body as object).sort()).toEqual(['event', 'userId']); // exactly these two (the route is additionalProperties: false)
    });

    it('14.604 a 429 becomes a sentence naming the wait; any other status is not this mapper\'s; postJson leaves a 429 as it was', async () => {
      const limited = new HttpError(429, { error: 'rate_limited', retryAfterSeconds: 42 });
      expect(rateLimitedError(limited, 'Looking people up by email')!.message)
        .toBe('Looking people up by email is rate limited right now. Wait about 42 seconds and call this again; nothing was changed.');
      expect(rateLimitedError(new HttpError(429, { error: 'rate_limited', retryAfterSeconds: 1 }), 'X')!.message).toContain('about 1 second and');
      expect(rateLimitedError(new HttpError(429, {}), 'X')!.message).toContain('Wait a minute and');
      expect(rateLimitedError(new HttpError(500, {}), 'X')).toBeNull(); // CONTROL
      // postJson itself, not the constructor: a 429 still comes out of it as the untouched HttpError (the sentence this replaces).
      await expect(postJson(throwing(429, { error: 'rate_limited', retryAfterSeconds: 42 }), '/x', {})).rejects.toMatchObject({ status: 429, message: 'gisik-nokma answered 429' });
    });
  });
  ```

  Kills (14.604):
  - first `it`: (a) `putJson` calls `session.put` without `withWords` → `viaPut` is `gisik-nokma answered 400`; (b) `wordsFor` drops the `${b['error']}: ` prefix → row 1 reads `Not a preferences document.` through both doors; (c) `wordsFor` handles every status (drops the `400`/`422` guard) → the 404/429/500 legs lose `status`; (d) `putJson` sends `session.post` → the CONTROL route `PUT /x` is never asked and the call 404s.
  - second `it`: (a) `postOutsider` rethrows the 422 instead of returning `wrapped` → the refusal row rejects; (b) it returns `raw` instead of `raw.result` → `toEqual(COMMITTED)` fails; (c) it drops `encodeURIComponent(workspaceId)` → the `W 1` request misses its route; (d) it posts `{ userId, event, workspaceId }` → the exact-keys leg fails.
  - third `it`: (a) the `seconds === 1` singular arm is dropped → `about 1 seconds`; (b) the `seconds === null ? 'a minute'` arm is dropped → a body without `retryAfterSeconds` reads `about null seconds` and `Wait a minute` fails; (c) `wordsFor` routes a 429 through `rateLimitedError` → postJson's 429 message changes.

  `apps/server/test/mcp/helpers/sweep2.ts` (shared by every hosted test of this plan; no case of its own, exercised by T2's neighbours and T4-T8):

  ```ts
  import { expect } from 'vitest';
  import { newUlid, type SubmittedEvent } from '@gisik-nokma/domain';
  import type { FastifyInstance } from 'fastify';
  import type { TestDatabase } from '../../helpers/db.js';

  /** Anyone with a session token: a `Caller` of `door-matrix.ts`, or an email account of `email-world.ts`. */
  export type Actor = { userId: string; sessionToken: string };
  type Result = { status: string; reason?: string; rule?: string };

  /** One event as the app's own clients send it (`origin: 'web'`), as `buildDoorWorld` builds them (`door-matrix.ts:55-56`). */
  export function event(workspaceId: string, actor: Actor, entity: SubmittedEvent['entity'], type: string, payload: unknown, id: string = newUlid()): SubmittedEvent {
    return { id, workspaceId, entity, type, version: 1, payload, actorId: actor.userId, origin: 'web', clientTime: new Date().toISOString() };
  }

  /** `POST /v1/events` as `actor`, through the session token (not the hosted door): the results, index-aligned. */
  export async function submitAs(app: FastifyInstance, actor: Actor, events: SubmittedEvent[]): Promise<Result[]> {
    const res = await app.inject({ method: 'POST', url: '/v1/events', headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${actor.sessionToken}` }, payload: { events } });
    return (res.json() as { results: Result[] }).results;
  }

  /** `submitAs`, asserting every event committed (a setup step that fails here fails with the server's own reason). */
  export async function mustCommit(app: FastifyInstance, actor: Actor, events: SubmittedEvent[]): Promise<void> {
    for (const r of await submitAs(app, actor, events)) expect(r.status, `${r.rule ?? ''} ${r.reason ?? ''}`).toBe('committed');
  }

  /** Change someone's workspace role the way `manage_members change_role` does (`member-admin-tools.ts:125`): `membership.granted` with the new role. */
  export function grantRole(app: FastifyInstance, owner: Actor, workspaceId: string, userId: string, role: 'admin' | 'member' | 'guest'): Promise<void> {
    return mustCommit(app, owner, [event(workspaceId, owner, { kind: 'membership', id: `${workspaceId}:${userId}` }, 'membership.granted', { userId, role })]);
  }

  /** The number of ledger events on one entity (optionally of one type). */
  export async function eventCount(db: TestDatabase, entityId: string, type?: string): Promise<number> {
    const { rows } = await db.pool.query<{ n: number }>(
      `SELECT count(*)::int AS n FROM events WHERE entity_id = $1 AND ($2::text IS NULL OR type = $2)`, [entityId, type ?? null]);
    return rows[0]!.n;
  }

  /** A note's projected body (`proj_notes.body`, jsonb). */
  export async function noteBody(db: TestDatabase, noteId: string): Promise<unknown> {
    const { rows } = await db.pool.query<{ body: unknown }>(`SELECT body FROM proj_notes WHERE note_id = $1`, [noteId]);
    return rows[0]?.body;
  }
  ```

  ```ts
  export async function confirmOrPreview(
    expected: string,
    given: string | undefined,
    describe: () => Promise<{ sentence: string; counts?: Record<string, number> }>,
  ```

  `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/submit.ts`: export the type (`export type EventResult = …`, same three members), then replace `submitOne`, `postJson` and append the new helpers. `submitBatch`, `envelopeFor` stay as they are.

  ```ts
  /** The sentence an ingest result that did not commit is spelled with; `null` for a commit. One spelling for every reader below. */
  export function resultError(result: EventResult): Error | null {
    // VERBATIM, as `submitOne` always spelled it: the rule's name and the reason's own sentence.
    if (result.status === 'refused') return new Error(`refused by ${result.rule}: ${result.reason}`);
    if (result.status === 'invalid') return new Error(`${result.code}: ${result.message}`);
    return null;
  }

  export async function submitOne(session: Session, event: SubmittedEvent): Promise<unknown> {
    const body = (await session.postEvents([event])) as { results: EventResult[] };
    const result = body.results[0];
    if (result === undefined) {
      throw new Error('gisik-nokma returned no result for the submitted event');
    }
    const failure = resultError(result);
    if (failure !== null) throw failure;
    return result;
  }

  const KNOWN_STATUSES = new Set(['committed', 'invalid', 'refused']);
  function isEventResult(value: unknown): value is EventResult {
    return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && KNOWN_STATUSES.has((value as { status?: unknown }).status as string);
  }

  /**
   * Several events in ONE `POST /v1/events`, every outcome returned index-aligned and NONE thrown (MCP sweep 2 PL-2).
   * `POST /v1/events` commits each event in its own transaction, so a refusal in the middle leaves its neighbours
   * committed (`ingest/ingest-service.ts:331-340`); `submitBatch` throws at the first one and would report an error
   * for a batch that had in part succeeded. This throws only when the ANSWER cannot be trusted (a rejected call, a
   * short, holed or missing `results`), with `whenUnknown` in front: nothing may then be claimed either way.
   */
  export async function submitBatchByIndex(session: Session, events: SubmittedEvent[], whenUnknown: string): Promise<EventResult[]> {
    let results: unknown;
    try {
      results = ((await session.postEvents(events)) as { results?: unknown } | null)?.results;
    } catch (error) {
      throw new Error(`${whenUnknown} (${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)})`);
    }
    if (!Array.isArray(results) || results.length !== events.length || !results.every(isEventResult)) {
      throw new Error(`${whenUnknown} (the answer did not carry one result for each of the ${events.length} events)`);
    }
    return results;
  }

  /** A 400 or 422 from a non-event route in the server's own words; `null` for any other status (rethrown untouched). */
  function wordsFor(error: HttpError): Error | null {
    if (error.status !== 400 && error.status !== 422) return null;
    const b = (error.body ?? {}) as Record<string, unknown>;
    if (b['status'] === 'refused') return new Error(`refused by ${String(b['rule'])}: ${String(b['reason'])}`);
    if (b['status'] === 'invalid') return new Error(`${String(b['code'])}: ${String(b['message'])}`);
    if (typeof b['message'] === 'string') {
      return new Error(typeof b['error'] === 'string' ? `${b['error']}: ${b['message']}` : b['message']);
    }
    return new Error(`gisik-nokma answered ${error.status}: ${JSON.stringify(error.body)}`);
  }

  async function withWords(call: () => Promise<unknown>): Promise<unknown> {
    try {
      return await call();
    } catch (error) {
      throw (error instanceof HttpError ? wordsFor(error) : null) ?? error;
    }
  }

  /** `session.post` to a route that is not the event door, with a 400 or 422 turned into the server's own words (unchanged behaviour). */
  export function postJson(session: Session, path: string, body: unknown): Promise<unknown> {
    return withWords(() => session.post(path, body));
  }

  /** The same for `session.put`. */
  export function putJson(session: Session, path: string, body: unknown): Promise<unknown> {
    return withWords(() => session.put(path, body));
  }

  /**
   * `POST /v1/workspaces/:id/assign-outsider { userId, event }` (`routes/people.ts:112-221`). The route nests its
   * ingest result under `result`, on 200 AND on a 422 refusal, so the refusal is returned here as an answer and never
   * thrown (as the app's gateway does, `packages/app-core/src/online/people.ts:48-63`): the caller spells it with
   * `resultError`. A 404 (the caller is not a member) is rethrown as it came.
   */
  export async function postOutsider(session: Session, workspaceId: string, userId: string, event: SubmittedEvent): Promise<EventResult> {
    const path = `/v1/workspaces/${encodeURIComponent(workspaceId)}/assign-outsider`;
    let raw: unknown;
    try {
      raw = await session.post(path, { userId, event });
    } catch (error) {
      if (error instanceof HttpError && error.status === 422) {
        const wrapped = (error.body as { result?: unknown } | null)?.result;
        if (isEventResult(wrapped)) return wrapped;
      }
      throw (error instanceof HttpError ? wordsFor(error) : null) ?? error;
    }
    const result = (raw as { result?: unknown } | null)?.result;
    if (!isEventResult(result)) throw new Error('gisik-nokma answered the outsider assignment without a result');
    return result;
  }

  /** A 429 as a sentence an assistant can act on (the generic `HttpError` message is only "answered 429"); `null` for any other status. */
  export function rateLimitedError(error: HttpError, what: string): Error | null {
    if (error.status !== 429) return null;
    const wait = (error.body as { retryAfterSeconds?: unknown } | null)?.retryAfterSeconds;
    const seconds = typeof wait === 'number' && wait > 0 ? Math.ceil(wait) : null;
    const when = seconds === null ? 'a minute' : `about ${seconds} second${seconds === 1 ? '' : 's'}`;
    return new Error(`${what} is rate limited right now. Wait ${when} and call this again; nothing was changed.`);
  }
  ```

  Keep the existing docblocks on `submitOne`, `submitBatch` and `postJson` above their new bodies; the VERBATIM comment moved into `resultError`.

  ```ts
  import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
  import { HttpError } from '../src/http-client.js';
  import { readTools } from '../src/tools/read-tools.js';
  import { recordingSession } from './fixtures/recording-session.js';

  const WS = 'W1';
  const T = 'T1';
  const TICKET = `/v1/workspaces/${WS}/tickets/${T}`;
  const NOTES = `${TICKET}/notes`;
  const DETAIL = { key: 'CCSO-4', title: 'Chiller', ticketState: { kind: 'ticket', id: T, fields: {} }, rollup: null };
  /** What the notes route answers per note (`NoteSummary`, `packages/domain/src/read-models.ts:883-894`): more than `get_ticket` shows. */
  const note = (n: number) => ({
    noteId: `N${n}`, workspaceId: WS, homeProjectId: '', authorId: `U${n}`, title: `Note ${n}`, scope: { mode: 'workspace' },
    attachedTickets: [{ ticketId: T, projectId: 'P', key: 'CCSO-4', title: 'Chiller' }], deleted: false,
    createdAt: '2026-10-01T00:00:00.000Z', updatedAt: `2026-10-02T00:00:${String(59 - (n % 60)).padStart(2, '0')}.000Z`,
  });
  const tool = (session: ReturnType<typeof recordingSession>['session']) => {
    const found = readTools(session).find((t) => t.name === 'get_ticket');
    if (found === undefined) throw new Error('no get_ticket');
    return found;
  };
  const paths = (requests: ReturnType<typeof recordingSession>['requests']) => requests.map((r) => `${r.method} ${r.path}`);

  describe('get_ticket and the notes attached to the ticket', () => {
    it('14.605 reads the notes only after the ticket read succeeded: a ticket the caller cannot see answers the uniform sentence and the notes route is never called', async () => {
      const hidden = recordingSession({
        routes: { [`GET ${TICKET}`]: () => { throw new HttpError(404, { error: 'not_found' }); }, [`GET ${NOTES}`]: { notes: [note(1)] } },
      });
      await expect(tool(hidden.session).handler({ workspaceId: WS, ticketId: T })).rejects.toThrow('ticket not found or not visible to you');
      expect(paths(hidden.requests)).toEqual([`GET ${TICKET}`]);
      // CONTROL, same `it`: a visible ticket makes both reads, the ticket FIRST.
      const visible = recordingSession({ routes: { [`GET ${TICKET}`]: DETAIL, [`GET ${NOTES}`]: { notes: [note(1)] } } });
      await tool(visible.session).handler({ workspaceId: WS, ticketId: T });
      expect(paths(visible.requests)).toEqual([`GET ${TICKET}`, `GET ${NOTES}`]);
    });

    it('14.606 answers the route\'s notes trimmed to noteId, title, authorId and updatedAt, cut at 50 with notesTotal counting them all; the description says what notes is and where the rest is read', async () => {
      const many = Array.from({ length: 60 }, (_, i) => note(i + 1));
      const s = recordingSession({ routes: { [`GET ${TICKET}`]: DETAIL, [`GET ${NOTES}`]: { notes: many } } });
      const out = (await tool(s.session).handler({ workspaceId: WS, ticketId: T })) as { notes: Array<Record<string, unknown>>; notesTotal: number };
      expect(out.notes).toHaveLength(50);
      expect(out.notesTotal).toBe(60);
      expect(Object.keys(out.notes[0]!).sort()).toEqual(['authorId', 'noteId', 'title', 'updatedAt']);
      expect(out.notes.map((n) => n['noteId'])).toEqual(many.slice(0, 50).map((n) => n.noteId)); // the route's order is kept
      // CONTROL, same `it`: a short list is whole, and the ticket's own members are still there.
      const few = recordingSession({ routes: { [`GET ${TICKET}`]: DETAIL, [`GET ${NOTES}`]: { notes: [note(1), note(2)] } } });
      const small = (await tool(few.session).handler({ workspaceId: WS, ticketId: T })) as Record<string, unknown> & { notes: unknown[] };
      expect(small.notes).toHaveLength(2);
      expect(small['notesTotal']).toBe(2);
      expect(small['key']).toBe('CCSO-4');
      const description = tool(s.session).description;
      // Each leg on its own clause: a bare `toContain('notes')` passes on `notesTotal` alone, and `/can read/` on the same clause.
      expect(description).toContain('`notes` lists the notes attached to it that you can read');
      expect(description).toContain('`notesTotal` counts them all');
      expect(description).toContain('read one in full with get_note');

    it('14.609 a failure of the notes read fails the whole call; the ticket is never returned with notes: [] standing for "no notes"', async () => {
      const broken = recordingSession({ routes: { [`GET ${TICKET}`]: DETAIL, [`GET ${NOTES}`]: () => { throw new HttpError(500, { error: 'boom' }); } } });

      const odd = recordingSession({ routes: { [`GET ${TICKET}`]: DETAIL, [`GET ${NOTES}`]: { nope: true } } });
      await expect(tool(odd.session).handler({ workspaceId: WS, ticketId: T })).rejects.toThrow(/not a list of notes/);
      // CONTROL, same `it`: an honest empty list is answered as one.
      const none = recordingSession({ routes: { [`GET ${TICKET}`]: DETAIL, [`GET ${NOTES}`]: { notes: [] } } });
      expect(await tool(none.session).handler({ workspaceId: WS, ticketId: T })).toMatchObject({ notes: [], notesTotal: 0 });
    });
  });
  ```

  ```ts
  import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
  import { newUlid } from '@gisik-nokma/domain';
  import { createTestDatabase, type TestDatabase } from '../helpers/db.js';
  import { buildDoorWorld, callTool } from './helpers/door-matrix.js';
  import { event, mustCommit } from './helpers/sweep2.js';

  /**
   * MCP sweep 2 T3, cases 14.607 and 14.608 (the package cases 14.605, 14.606 and 14.609 are
   * `packages/mcp-server/test/get-ticket-notes.test.ts`). Every call is a `tools/call` on `POST /mcp`. Run LOCALLY.
   */
  let db: TestDatabase;
  beforeAll(async () => {
    db = await createTestDatabase();
  });
  afterAll(async () => {
    await db.drop();
  });

  type Notes = { notes: Array<{ noteId: string; title: string }>; notesTotal: number };

  describe('get_ticket notes through the hosted door', () => {
    it('14.607 the notes are the ones THIS caller may read: the author sees a private note and the member does not, nor a count of it; a stranger gets the uniform sentence', async () => {
      const w = await buildDoorWorld(db, '2607');
      // A third note every member can read but that is NOT attached: without it, "the ticket's notes" and "every note I can
      // read" are the same two notes, and a tool that read the workspace's list would pass.
      const looseNoteId = newUlid();
      await mustCommit(w.app, w.owner, [
        event(w.workspaceId, w.owner, { kind: 'note', id: looseNoteId }, 'note.created', { title: 'Loose matrix note', homeProjectId: '', scope: { mode: 'workspace' } }),
      ]);
      for (const noteId of [w.privateNoteId, w.workspaceNoteId]) {
        const attached = await callTool(w.app, w.owner.token, 'link_note', { noteId, ticketId: w.ticketId, action: 'attach' });
        expect(attached.isError, attached.text).toBe(false);
      }
      const ask = (token: string) => callTool(w.app, token, 'get_ticket', { workspaceId: w.workspaceId, ticketId: w.ticketId });
      const ownerRead = await ask(w.owner.token);
      const owner = ownerRead.json as Notes;
      expect(owner.notes.map((n) => n.noteId).sort()).toEqual([w.privateNoteId, w.workspaceNoteId].sort());
      expect(owner.notesTotal).toBe(2); // CONTROL, same `it`: the author sees both
      expect(ownerRead.text).not.toContain(looseNoteId); // only ATTACHED notes, not every note the caller can read
      const memberRead = await ask(w.member.token);
      const member = memberRead.json as Notes;
      expect(member.notes.map((n) => n.noteId)).toEqual([w.workspaceNoteId]);
      expect(member.notesTotal).toBe(1); // no "1 hidden", no count of the note the member cannot read
      expect(memberRead.text).not.toContain(w.privateNoteId);
      expect(memberRead.text).not.toContain('Private matrix note');
      expect(memberRead.text).not.toContain(looseNoteId);
      const stranger = await ask(w.stranger.token);
      expect(stranger.isError).toBe(true);
      expect(stranger.text).toBe('ticket not found or not visible to you');
    });

    it('14.608 a deleted note is absent from the ticket and is back after restore_note', async () => {
      const w = await buildDoorWorld(db, '2608');
      await callTool(w.app, w.owner.token, 'link_note', { noteId: w.workspaceNoteId, ticketId: w.ticketId, action: 'attach' });
      const read = async () => (await callTool(w.app, w.owner.token, 'get_ticket', { workspaceId: w.workspaceId, ticketId: w.ticketId })).json as Notes;
      expect((await read()).notesTotal).toBe(1);
      const removed = await callTool(w.app, w.owner.token, 'delete_note', { noteId: w.workspaceNoteId, confirm_name: 'Matrix note' });
      expect(removed.isError, removed.text).toBe(false);
      expect(await read()).toMatchObject({ notes: [], notesTotal: 0 });
      const back = await callTool(w.app, w.owner.token, 'restore_note', { noteId: w.workspaceNoteId });
      expect(back.isError, back.text).toBe(false);
      const again = await read();
      expect(again.notes.map((n) => n.noteId)).toEqual([w.workspaceNoteId]);
      expect(again.notes[0]!.title).toBe('Matrix note');
    });
  });
  ```

  In `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/read-tools.ts`, add above `readTools` (beside `NO_SCOPE`):

  ```ts
  /** `get_ticket` shows at most this many attached notes; `notesTotal` counts every one the caller can read (MCP sweep 2 §3.2). */
  const MAX_TICKET_NOTES = 50;
  const notesUnread = (why: string): string =>
    `The ticket was read but the notes attached to it could not be (${why}), so nothing is returned: an empty list would read as "no notes".`;

  type AttachedNote = { noteId: string; title: string; authorId: string; updatedAt: string };

  /**
   * The notes attached to a ticket that THIS caller may read, as the Notes section of the ticket sheet reads them
   * (`GET …/tickets/:id/notes`, `routes/notes.ts:87-96`). The route has no ticket check of its own, so the caller reads the
   * ticket first. Any failure here fails the call (GT-5).
   */
  async function attachedNotes(session: Session, path: string): Promise<AttachedNote[]> {
    let body: unknown;
    try {
      body = await session.get(path);
    } catch (error) {
      throw new Error(notesUnread(error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)));
    }
    const list = (body as { notes?: unknown } | null)?.notes;
    if (!Array.isArray(list)) throw new Error(notesUnread('the answer was not a list of notes'));
    return (list as AttachedNote[]).map((n) => ({ noteId: n.noteId, title: n.title, authorId: n.authorId, updatedAt: n.updatedAt }));
  }
  ```

  Replace the `get_ticket` description and handler (the `list_members` tool after it is untouched):

  ```ts
      description:
        'Read one ticket in full: its visibility row, its folded state (assignee, collaborators, ' +

        '`notesTotal` counts them all; read one in full with get_note. ' +
        'A ticket you cannot see is indistinguishable from one that does not exist — both answer not_found.',

        const path = `/v1/workspaces/${encodeURIComponent(parsed.workspaceId)}/tickets/${encodeURIComponent(parsed.ticketId)}`;
        const detail = (await readOrNotFound(session, path, 'ticket')) as { ticketState?: { fields?: Record<string, { value: unknown }> } } & Record<string, unknown>;
        // AFTER the ticket read: the notes route does not check the ticket (GT-1).
        const notes = await attachedNotes(session, `${path}/notes`);

          description: bodyAsMarkdown(description?.value ?? null),

  ```
